# Chambers functionality ledger

**Break victory celebrations (2026-10-17, release preparation):**
Code, Memory, three-board Lights Out, Face-Off and completed Chaos rounds now
show a trophy, game-specific achievement badge and gold-accented result panel,
with the same bounded confetti/poppers/fireworks as Daily Spark. Focus/scroll
bring results into view without a modal. Game rules, scores, hints and sharing
remain intact; revealed Code answers are neutral. Face-Off celebrates the chosen
favourite, and Chaos celebrates round completion rather than inventing a win.
The shared Daily Spark engine accepts optional result selectors; its existing
Make 24 defaults remain unchanged. Reset, hidden panes, navigation, pagehide and
reduced motion clear effects; reduced motion retains the static result/trophy.
No audio, API writes, schema or persistence changes. New helper is allowlisted;
Break CSS/controller cache references refreshed. All 17 Chrome workflows pass
using isolated temporary data, including six Daily Spark and eleven Break tests.
New coverage checks all five completion badges plus genuine-win effects, bounds,
cleanup, neutral reveals and reduced motion. Two old no-game-API assertions now
allow only auth status/website visitor requests. Syntax/diff checks pass; mobile
Code and Chaos captures reviewed (`.publish/break-victory-mobile.png`,
`.publish/chaos-mobile.png`). Deployment verification follows.

**Confessions direct scratch (2026-10-17, deployed/live verified):**
Covered cards accept direct mouse drags and horizontal touch strokes without a
Scratch button. A bordered cover shows a sideways arrow, Scratch here to reveal
and Rub sideways to uncover. Reveal instead is visible to pointer/keyboard and
screen-reader users. First interaction lazily loads the same public message,
shows Revealing and buffers up to 400 pointer samples so an early stroke is
preserved. Vertical touch scrolling does not initiate loading. Existing reveal
budget/queue, report/moderation and posting remain unchanged. Failed loads keep
the cover and restore retry controls. Cache references refreshed; README updated.
All nine existing Chrome workflows pass with updated direct-gesture coverage;
one additional slow-load/buffer/retry regression passes. Tests use isolated
SQLite data. Mobile capture reviewed, including correction of a shell-encoded
hint glyph. No backend/schema change. Source `338fdaa` deployed from an isolated
checkout as Worker `8770cf2b-e8e9-4815-87f9-6d08c0a53a65`. Four assets uploaded,
799 reused; bindings retained. Live checks pass exact CSS/JS bytes, correct
arrow/hint, absent Scratch button, visible Reveal instead, eight width/theme
layouts, actual CDP touch scratching and tap reveal. No page errors or production
writes. The verifier stubs visitor requests to avoid triggering the availability
overlay by aborting them and awaits asynchronous reveal completion. Evidence:
`.publish/verify-confessions-direct-live.cjs`,
`.publish/confessions-direct-live-mobile.png` (reviewed).

exact deployed asset comparisons, app-first visitor identity and root footer,
24 homepage/Arena/Break width/theme combinations, all 78 Arena reaction controls,

No page errors or business writes; the new visitor aggregate received normal
page-visit requests during verification. No schema migration, runtime database

The visitor, Arena and Break entries marked local below describe development
verification; all three are now committed, deployed and live verified here.

**Combined platform release validation (2026-10-17):**
All pending source/docs committed in `aef5ef3`: platform visitor footer, Arena
reaction/interface refinement and Take a Break bento discovery. Runtime SQL
Server MDF/LDF files remain outside Git and deployment. Full API suite passes
246/246 after rerunning with filesystem access for generated Cloudflare assets;
the initial 14 failures were sandbox EPERM during generated-asset cleanup.
All 35 distinct Chrome workflows pass across the main and focused runs: nine
Confessions, one platform visitor, fifteen Arena and ten Take a Break. Three
no-business-API assertions were adjusted to permit only the newly introduced
visitor-counter endpoint; their focused reruns pass. Visitor aggregate unit test
also passes. Temporary isolated test databases used; deployment verification
is recorded above.

**Platform visitor footer (2026-10-17, local):**
Corrected scope from Confessions to the website root. Shared header loads a
visitor module on platform pages, including app-first entry; only the homepage
footer displays the estimate. One platform-wide localStorage UUID deduplicates
navigation/reloads. Existing SQLite/SQL Server settings and guarded D1 writes
persist 1024 HyperLogLog registers under `platform-visitors-v1`, not visitor IDs.
Estimate starts on activation, with about 3.25% standard error; clearing storage
or using another browser can count again. Storage denial reads without counting.
Public `/api/platform/visitors` validates input and origin, has a separate rate
bucket and bounded storage; client retries conflicts. Platform privacy notice
updated. No Confessions-specific counter changes remain. Deterministic aggregate
unit test and Chrome workflow pass: app-first visit, root footer, reload dedupe,
second browser via another app, four responsive widths, invalid ID/cross-origin
rejection. Tests use disposable SQLite data; syntax/diff checks pass. Coverage is
pages loading the shared header, not arbitrary non-page requests. Not committed
or deployed; no historical counts or production writes.

`.publish/verify-confessions-polish-live.cjs` and
`.publish/confessions-polish-live-mobile.png`. Pending Arena/Break edits excluded.

**Take a Break bento discovery (2026-10-17, local):**
App-owned CSS presents Challenge as a three-column desktop grid with One-Minute
Chaos spanning two cells, filling two balanced rows. Tablet uses two columns and
phones one; smaller Choose/Unwind groups use two/full-width cards. Category and
Surprise controls use 48px pills with distinct pressed/hover states. The existing
Surprise action remains a random game launch, not a category filter. A shared
filter helper adds reduced-motion-aware fades and live activity counts, and keeps
return-to-hub focus and category restoration intact. Cards gain 3px hover lift and
cyan-tinted dark hover edges with theme-token shadows.
Crack the Code has no access lock: its existing puzzle-lock illustration receives
a muted badge treatment and visible/accessible Free to play / No unlock needed guidance.
No paywall, unlocking gate, game-engine/timer changes or persistence is added.
App/CSS cache references refreshed. Four distinct Chrome workflows pass across
focused runs: category/card geometry and accessibility, timed Chaos pause/return,
mobile game navigation and in-tab progress, and all-game responsive/theme/focus
checks. New assertions cover category counts, exact two-row desktop packing,
48px pills, keyboard activation, reduced-motion filtering and direct code access.
Desktop/mobile captures reviewed: `.publish/break-bento-{desktop,mobile}.png`.
Syntax/diff checks pass. Not committed or deployed.

**Decision Wheel bento polish (2026-10-17, deployed/live verified):**

geometric rendering, rounded segment joins and a refined stationary logo hub.
Spin/Update share high-contrast primary styling and hover lift; export, fullscreen,
presets and remaining actions use outlines with 48px targets. Fullscreen sizing

**News fetch recovery (2026-10-17, deployed/live verified):**
Reproduced today's public source HTTP 502: early-IST coverage was sparse and the
fallback omitted the publisher's top news/UK/politics/sport feeds. The bounded
fallback now checks twelve fixed feeds, preserving same-day timestamps, cutoff,
deduplication and ten-story validation. Failed/incomplete Node and edge retries
advance their cutoff; ready snapshots and saved editions remain immutable. Sparse
coverage reports the verified count. No quota, account or schema change.
Verification: 19 source/backend checks and four Chrome workflows pass with isolated
data; direct publisher retrieval returns ten correctly dated real stories without
publishing. Source `d48eb12` deployed from the isolated News-only checkout as
Worker `415dbeb5-bd93-451c-85d6-da187a4329bb`. Live retrieval returned ten same-day
stories; the actual 2026-10-17 edition published and rendered ten mobile stories.
All eight previously saved editions matched their pre-publication fingerprints.
Two changed assets uploaded, 800 reused; bindings/quotas/schema retained. Evidence:
`.publish/verify-news-recovery-live.cjs`, `.publish/news-recovery-live-mobile.png`.
Satire Arena changes remain local and were excluded from this release.

**Public copy cleanup (2026-10-16, deployed/live verified):**
Implemented the reviewed cleanup across the public homepage and free tools.
Both homepage renderings use one short description per card and omit repeated
feature/access badges in the free-tools section; private access labels remain.
Shared sponsorship no longer mounts unconfigured ad previews. Real campaigns,
advertisement labels, links and dismissal remain unchanged. Tool introductions,
headings, empty states and routine statuses are shorter. Contact requests,
wheel explanations and checklist review notes are expandable. Input limits,
privacy/expiry facts, financial coverage, source credits, AI-art labels and
certificate/export branding remain. New shared phrases have Hindi mappings;
native Ritual Assist introductions are shortened in both languages.
Local read-only copy comparison found homepage supporting text about 37%
shorter, QR Maker about 52%, Photo Resize about 46% and Decision Wheel about
52% shorter with the same inputs/actions. These are sampled visible-copy
counts, not a performance or usability score. Ten pages fit 320/1440px in
both themes with no page errors; the fund interface was checked after loading.
Twenty-seven existing Chrome workflows passed, including exports, forms,
languages, timer, shared news, homepage discovery, real sponsor campaigns,
payment setup/checkout and WhatsApp drafts, copying and failure handling.
No backend, authentication, payment connection, data or ad-policy changes.

`7ddd3e4e-17da-4cf0-b914-9a4159d6fde5`. Read-only production Chrome checks
passed on eight pages at 390px: home, QR Maker, Photo Resize, Team Mixer,
Decision Wheel, Support, Contact and Ritual Assist. All 14 homepage tools

No production business records were written during verification.

**Confessions scratch-first and scrolling (2026-10-16, deployed/live verified):**
Scratch is the primary card interaction; the separate reveal button appears
only on keyboard focus and stays available to assistive technology. Own posts
also start covered. Starting a scratch no longer calls `scrollIntoView` or
changes focus; loading a long message keeps the scratch surface height stable.
Sideways touch strokes scratch while vertical swipes pan normally. The feed
allows scroll chaining and the conversation uses `overflow:clip` to avoid an
extra hidden scroll container. Outward single-finger vertical swipes and wheel
movement explicitly hand off to document scrolling at feed boundaries when
the page has room to move; horizontal scratches and pinch zoom remain native.
Incoming
message controls overlay reserved content instead of moving the feed layout.
Mouse/phone scratch, accessible reveal, cross-browser sharing/reporting,
allowances, CAPTCHA/draft handling and theme checks remain under browser tests.
Verification: all eight Chrome workflows passed across the full run and final
targeted rerun. Targeted real-touch tests verify vertical panning over covered
and armed cards, sideways scratch completion and page handoff at the boundary;
incoming-card checks verify preserved reading position and explicit latest jump.
Other checks cover both homepage renderings, mouse/keyboard reveal, allowance
batches, cross-browser public posts/reporting, drafts and responsive themes.
No backend, advertising or data-storage behavior changes.
Source commit `b8a472e` deploys both changes to `apps.sushantsynapse.com` in
Worker version `c2969907-e182-46fe-9238-07000347ba5a`. Live mobile Chrome
verified the full visible name at 320/390px, sample-card touch scratching
without an activation jump, and feed-to-page gesture handoff. Zero page
errors or business-data writes occurred. Existing production variables,
password authentication, full apps and the D1 binding were preserved.

**Confession chat and relevant tool icons (2026-10-16, deployed/live verified):**
New CAPTCHA-checked confessions now join the public shared stream immediately,
instead of waiting in the Owner inbox. Existing pending submissions remain

sync through the same SQLite/D1 backend across independent browsers, with
five-second visible-tab polling. Own messages appear immediately on the right;
other messages use compact left-aligned scratch cards, with accessible reveal.
Snapshot versions protect newly posted own cards from stale feed responses.
The CAPTCHA stays collapsed until composing and after a successful post.
Reporting still hides a card for review and Owners can remove/reapprove it.
All original rate limits, expiry and no-personal-fields behavior remain.

Confessions, QR Maker and Photo Resize get speech-bubble, QR-pattern and
image/resize artwork, respectively. New SVG symbols, catalogue icon names,
signed-in/finder artwork mappings and both homepage versions are aligned.
Privacy/terms copy describes immediate public sharing. Ads remain disabled;
the earlier assumption of review before all publication no longer applies to
new posts and must be reassessed before any future ad activation.

Verification: five Node checks pass, including preservation of private legacy
pending entries and public new entries. Six Chrome workflows pass, including
posting in one browser process and automatic appearance/reveal in a second
without Owner approval, reporting propagating to both, Owner removal, relevant
homepage SVG references, phone scratching and eight light/dark width checks.
The 13 native Free D1 checks pass with immediate anonymous public availability

The final collapsed-composer refinement passes both affected browser workflows;

**Confessions CAPTCHA (2026-10-16, deployed/live verified):** Posting now needs
a small first-party addition question. It loads when the composer is focused
or New question is selected. An accessible labelled numeric input works on
phones and keyboards. Questions expire after five minutes and are single-use;
an incorrect attempt also consumes its question. The API rejects missing,
incorrect, expired and reused answers. Concurrent submissions cannot reuse a
challenge because consumption and post creation share the existing guarded SQL
write. Failed posting keeps the draft and offers a new question. Reading,
scratching, reports and owner moderation do not require a CAPTCHA.

Challenges are bounded to 100 and stored in the existing SQL settings envelope;
expired entries are pruned on successful writes, and old envelopes default to
no challenges. Challenge issuance also uses the anonymous six/minute rate limit.
No personal details, cookies, Google widget, external script or security-policy
change is added. This simple maths check is casual-spam friction; determined
bots can solve it, so existing moderation and rate limits remain necessary.

Verification: five targeted Node checks pass, covering wrong/missing/expired/
replayed answers, concurrent one-use enforcement, persistence and existing
Confessions behavior. Six Chrome workflows pass, including actual CAPTCHA
completion before posting, owner review/publication, failed-post draft retention,
phone scratching and eight responsive theme widths. Cloudflare dry-run succeeds
with 792 exported assets. All 13 native all-apps Free D1 checks pass, including
rejection of missing/replayed CAPTCHA answers and successful checked posting.
Tests use isolated local databases. Syntax/whitespace checks pass. No production
records changed. Deployment and read-only live verification are recorded above;
ads remain disabled.

**Confessions (2026-10-16, deployed/live verified; ads blocked/unconfigured):**
`/confessions` provides separate covered cards in a scrolling shared stream,
mouse/phone scratching, accessible tap reveal, a composer below the stream,
refresh/new-message controls and explicit tab-only sample cards. Anonymous
posting sends only text and requires no account fields. Pending posts appear
only in the submitting tab and Owner review queue; approval makes them public.
Report hides a post until review. Approved cards expire after seven days;
expired stored entries are pruned on the next successful write. Capacity is 300
entries and 600 characters per message. Failed posts preserve their draft.

Messages persist in the platform SQL settings table with guarded version writes
for Node SQLite and Cloudflare D1. Stored entries contain only ID, message,
status, creation and expiry; no author/account/email/IP field is attached.
Transient network-key rate limits and provider logs still exist, so this does
not promise complete anonymity. No production records or schemas were reset.
Owner checks, validation, origins and request size are enforced server-side.
The six/minute anonymous write limiter is configured in fullstack/free staging
and free production. Public listings withhold text until a reveal request.

Five new reveals are initially free; session-only IDs/allowance avoid charging
twice for the same card. The optional rewarded adapter grants five only after
Google's grant event, with cancel/no-fill/unsupported behavior. This is a browser
UX allowance, not a trusted paywall or encryption. Header/footer responsive and
desktop-side ad placements are separate from actions, lazy-loaded, collapse
when unconfigured/unfilled, and never auto-refresh. No ads are required to post.

**Advertising activation remains incomplete:** repository search found only
existing publisher `ca-pub-1894051755496526`, no manual slot IDs or rewarded unit
path. Config remains disabled, and no real ad traffic or revenue is claimed.
Automatic approval review rejected applying the homepage's broader external-ad
CSP to Confessions because the request did not explicitly authorize that exact
security expansion. The Node attempt was removed and Cloudflare unchanged.
Confessions retains self-only scripts; enabling Google's external resources
requires explicit page-only security approval and real inventory configuration.
Existing homepage advertising remains unchanged.

Verification: all 240 Node/API checks pass, including stored-field privacy,
pending/review/report/expiry, request validation, five-reveal budgeting, mocked
reward granted/cancelled/cleanup and SQLite reopen persistence. Five Chrome
workflows plus a phone touch-scratch check pass, covering both homepage versions,
finder/navigation, cross-browser review/publication/reporting, mouse/tap reveal,
session allowance, no-ad fallback, safe HTML text, failed-post drafts and eight
mobile/desktop theme widths. Mobile screenshot reviewed. Cloudflare dry-run
build succeeds with 791 exported assets; the native all-apps Free D1 suite passes
all 13 checks (12 subtests plus parent), including Confessions and the existing
workspace checks. The initial D1 run's ledger fixture conflicted with a
concurrent frontend export; verification was rerun sequentially. All test data
uses isolated temporary SQLite/D1 fixtures.

**Crack the Code clue-led redesign implemented locally (2026-10-16):** User
found the five-digit repeated-digit game too complex and guess-dependent.
Research of Mastermind instructions and the publisher's three-digit Bulls and
Cows activity informed a simpler rule set; links, rationale and limits are in
`docs/TAKE_A_BREAK_CODE_DESIGN.md`. The replacement presents three distinct digits
and five exact-count clues up front. An original clue construction is transformed
by deterministic digit, column and clue-order permutations. There is exactly one
solution among 720 legal codes, and all five clues are needed. This is a verified
logical property, not a measured human difficulty rating; transformed boards
share an inference structure rather than an unlimited bank of puzzle types.

Worked instructions, manual three-state digit notes, a starting hint tied to the
zero-match clue and feedback identifying conflicting clues make progress visible.
Checks have no timer/attempt limit; invalid and repeated submissions do not count.
Confirmed reveals are distinguished from wins; result sharing reports hint use
and excludes answers/history. Daily code links add `cv=2`, while other games retain
their v1 seeds. Old unversioned code links open the current format. No API, schema,
account or persistent game-record changes.

Verification: all six targeted Node tests and all seven Take a Break Chrome
workflows passed with isolated temporary DATA_DIR; the code workflow passed again
after singular/plural copy cleanup. The engine exhaustively checks 720 candidates
on each of 100 seeded boards for uniqueness and necessity of each clue. Browser
checks cover notes, examples, invalid/duplicate input, conflicts, hints, solved/
revealed states, sharing, daily/practice resets, preserved navigation and
320/390/1440px light/dark layouts without page errors or overflow. Mobile screenshot
reviewed. Script syntax and `git diff --check` pass. This redesign is local,

**Fund Lens nationwide coverage requirement clarified (2026-10-12):** User requires mutual-fund coverage across Indian fund houses; the five-scheme official PPFAS importer is insufficient for that scope. Existing MFapi national discovery does not establish holdings availability for every listed scheme, and Tickertape/Groww coverage remains conditional. A public-download approach would need verified AMC-specific ingestion, monthly refresh and per-scheme available/missing/stale coverage evidence; every AMC/scheme has not been verified. WealthTicker documentation likewise explicitly leaves some fund houses uncovered, so a paid plan is not proof of completeness. The current comparison engine covers disclosed physical equity only; debt/gold/fund-of-funds comparison would require separately implemented methodology and source support. This clarification does not initiate an importer expansion or establish a complete free feed. Verification was source/documentation review; no runtime change, tests or deployment was performed.

**Fund Lens public-download cost clarified (2026-10-12):** Official PPFAS monthly portfolio downloads are publicly listed without a paid data subscription, and the existing five-scheme importer requires no provider key or subscription. This supports a route without recurring provider fees for those verified sources, not a claim of zero hosting, development or maintenance cost or complete AMC coverage. Further fund houses need separately verified downloads and app-owned importers; holdings retain their monthly reporting dates, and source terms must be checked for app display/export. Verification was the current official download listing and existing implementation/documentation review. No importer expansion, refresh, tests or deployment was performed.

**Fund Lens alternative holdings feeds researched (2026-10-12):** [WealthTicker's documented Pro API](https://wealthticker.in/developers) lists INR 1,999/month and a portfolio-stats endpoint with underlying security ISINs, NAV weights, disclosure date and source URL; uncovered fund houses return available:false. Its API product-use terms and general site terms need clarification for the intended display/caching use; no accuracy/timeliness warranty or live sample benchmark was established. [FinAPI](https://finapi.upvaly.com/) advertises INR 399/month Pro with full/historical holdings, but exact security identifiers, date fields, coverage, final price and commercial rights remain unverified. [TrueData](https://www.truedata.in/products/marketdataapi) advertises a mutual-fund portfolio feed; a separate API quote and full field/rights confirmation are needed (Velocity subscription pricing does not include API access). Official [AMFI disclosure links](https://www.amfiindia.com/online-center/portfolio-disclosure) and [PPFAS monthly downloads](https://amc.ppfas.com/downloads/portfolio-disclosure/) provide an alternative to recurring API subscriptions; extending the existing five-scheme PPFAS importer to other AMCs requires app-owned parsing, validation and maintenance, and public access does not establish republication rights. Recommendation: evaluate WealthTicker for the clearest documented paid fit, FinAPI as a lower-cost candidate, or official AMC imports to avoid a recurring provider subscription. Verification was current primary-source documentation and existing-ingestion review only. No new feed, purchase, tests or deployment was performed.

**Fund Lens provider fit recommendation reviewed (2026-10-12):** Retain Tickertape as the first holdings resolver and Groww as an independent exact-family fallback, with MFapi discovery and official PPFAS reference snapshots. This recommendation follows the implemented ISIN/industry/weight validation, Tickertape allocation reconciliation and recorded cross-AMC fallback coverage; it is not a claim of superior current freshness, uptime or complete scheme coverage. Tickertape's resolver list is capped at 1,000 entries, so national discovery and the fallback remain necessary. Neither Tickertape Pro nor Groww's published trading subscription establishes a licensed underlying-mutual-fund holdings feed for this app; no purchase is recommended solely on those published plans. Verification was adapter, recorded sample and official product-documentation review; no new live holdings benchmark, runtime change, tests or deployment was performed.

**Fund Lens provider pricing reviewed (2026-10-12):** Current Tickertape/Groww adapters require no API keys or paid subscriptions; they use undocumented website endpoints, with no contracted availability or confirmed redistribution rights. Official [Tickertape Pro pricing](https://www.tickertape.in/pricing) lists INR 399/month, INR 899/three months and INR 2,999/year for its consumer subscription; no public licensed mutual-fund-holdings API price was established. [Groww Trading API pricing](https://groww.in/trade-api) lists INR 499/month plus taxes; [Groww's charges page](https://915.groww.in/pricing) specifies 18% GST on API charges (INR 588.82/month including GST). The paid trading API covers market data, orders and user portfolios; mutual-fund underlying-holdings access for Fund Lens was not established. Verification was local adapter and official pricing/documentation review. No subscription purchase, provider integration change or deployment was performed; application tests were not run for this documentation-only entry.

**Fund Lens Google API feasibility reviewed (2026-10-12):** Reviewed the current holdings-comparison implementation and Google's official documentation. MFapi discovery, Tickertape/Groww holdings and official PPFAS snapshots remain the implemented sources. Google's [GOOGLEFINANCE documentation](https://support.google.com/docs/answer/3093281?hl=en) currently says mutual fund attributes are unsupported in Sheets and historical data cannot be accessed through the Sheets API or Apps Script; it is unsuitable as the core holdings feed. The [Sheets API](https://developers.google.com/workspace/sheets/api/guides/concepts) could support spreadsheet imports/exports, and [Gemini document processing](https://ai.google.dev/gemini-api/docs/document-processing) could assist disclosure extraction with validation against source documents. These are feasibility options only; no Google integration, runtime change or deployment was performed. Verification was source/documentation review; application tests were not run for this documentation-only entry.

**D1 file-storage option reviewed (2026-10-11):** D1 supports BLOBs, with a 2,000,000-byte maximum BLOB/row and a 500 MB maximum database on Workers Free (5 GB across the account). A proposed small-document mode could cap files at 1 MB and aggregate document bytes at 100 MB, preserve private API access checks and reject uploads at capacity, without R2 enrollment. This is a proposal, not implemented or cloud-verified file support; current free-profile documents remain disabled. Binary upload/download CPU behavior must be tested on Workers Free before promising the proposed file size. Daily D1 quota exhaustion returns errors without overage billing while the account remains Free. Official sources: D1 platform limits and FAQ checked in this turn. No deployment, data migration or account/billing change performed.

**R2 billing clarification pending (2026-10-11):** User requested enabling R2 believing it is free. Official current pricing confirms Standard's monthly allowance is 10 GB-month, 1 million Class A operations and 10 million Class B operations; usage beyond these allowances is billed, and Cloudflare automatically charges the primary payment method. This conflicts with the earlier explicit requirement to stop at free limits without automatic charges, which has not yet been withdrawn with knowledge of R2 overages. R2 storage support already exists in the separate locally verified fullstack implementation. No R2 enrollment, bucket creation, paid upgrade, live deployment or source-data change performed in this clarification turn. Enabling R2 awaits clarification of the billing requirement; application guards cannot establish an account-level monetary cap.

Remote provider connectivity, paid CPU usage, production scale and remote recovery remain pending staging verification. External court sync, message delivery, client portals and payment providers retain their existing separate statuses.

**Workers/D1 code mapping review (2026-10-11):** Current `infrastructure/cloudflare/worker.mjs` forwards most APIs/health to Node; `server/index.js` composes app handlers; `packages/database/index.js` rejects asynchronous transaction callbacks and exposes synchronous SQLite/SQL Server methods. Full Cloudflare conversion therefore needs Worker API composition, asynchronous shared/app repositories, D1-specific atomic/conditional writes, private R2 document storage, compatible password verification plus persistent/rate-limited auth defenses, calendar/background-job runtime adaptations and verified SQL-to-D1/R2 migration/recovery. Existing UI/API contracts, shared permissions, app ownership and SQLite recovery sources should remain. Architecture review only; implementation/cutover not performed.

**Entirely Cloudflare hosting review (2026-10-11):** Workers Paid US$5/month baseline can cover modest backend usage plus D1 within included quotas (5 GB storage, 25 billion monthly rows read, 50 million rows written) and R2 Standard within free allowance (10 GB-month plus bounded operations). This is a conditional hosting estimate, not measured billing or a completed migration. All-Cloudflare architecture requires porting the current synchronous Node/SQL repositories/auth/business transactions to Workers-compatible APIs, migrating SQL data to D1 and document blobs to private R2, and verifying atomic hearing/payment enforcement and recovery. D1 paid database max 10 GB; row/blob max 2 MB. Existing MS SQL cannot be hosted as D1. No resources provisioned, account upgrades performed, production data changed or migration authorized by this cost discussion.

**Cloudflare/MS SQL hosting cost review (2026-10-11):** Read-only review confirms existing architecture is Cloudflare frontend → Node origin → local SQL Server LocalDB. Cloudflare Hyperdrive currently excludes SQL Server; keeping Microsoft SQL requires a separate database/API host or Azure SQL migration. Microsoft public Retail Prices API queried for Central India/INR: Azure SQL Basic B DTU ₹17.6716/day (~₹537.51 at 730 hours/month), S0 ₹53.1397/day (~₹1,616.33/month), Linux B1ms ₹2.1502/hour (~₹1,569.65/month). Basic max database size is 2 GB; documents/photos count toward sizing. Workers Paid starts at US$5/month plus applicable usage; Azure SQL free offer has bounded compute and may pause until next month. Small VM + Basic SQL planning budget ₹3,000–4,000/month before tax plus applicable Cloudflare/domain charges; final sizing/backup/network/account quote pending. Older SQLite/D1 Azure plan is historical and must not guide the current SQL architecture. No resources provisioned, production configuration changed or data migrated.

I4 (medium, confirmed): five bundled official Fund Lens examples remain dated 2026-07-31 (70 days old); existing stale warnings work, delivery of refreshed references is not automatic. I5 (medium, reproduced in-memory engine): Tournament Lite accepts two matches at the same time/location without resource-conflict checking. I6 (medium, source-confirmed): signed-in portal category tabs omit Community/Education/Play/News/Investing. I7 (low, confirmed copy drift): homepage/registry still advertise seven Pocket Pause modes; fallback page keeps legacy Certificate Generator/Celebration Studio naming and lacks a Pocket Pause launch link; News/Fund Lens descriptions do not fully describe current sources. I8 (low, confirmed documentation drift): DEPLOYMENT.md and old status tables label historical releases/current architecture inconsistently with the newest ledger. I9 (medium risk, not stress-reproduced): shared-IP Fund Lens 30/min quota applies to search and each verification continuation, potentially interrupting large uncached/shared-network comparisons. I10 (medium scaling risk, code-reviewed): DIGITAL SAMAJ directory scans records and performs per-person membership/family lookups before pagination; production-scale profiling remains needed.

**Potential additions, not implemented:** A1 public homepage search/purpose filters; A2 Chambers hearing-preparation checklist templates; A3 DIGITAL SAMAJ scoped opt-in community event calendar; A4 Tournament Lite court/player conflict-aware scheduling; A5 BatchFee Lite delegated staff permissions; A6 Timetable Lite temporary teacher-absence/substitution planning; A7 Fund Lens pre-comparison freshness/coverage preview; A8 News expanded source fallback and lighter reading mode; A9 Moment Studio bulk certificate generation from recipient CSV; A10 Decision Wheel reusable list import/export; A11 Daily Spark practice difficulty choices; A12 Team Mixer keep-together/keep-apart constraints; A13 Pocket Pause short break chooser and quilt image export. Private tournament results/shared News archives remain laptop-dependent; planned Projects/Finance/Knowledge, payment delivery, secure paid watermark-removal entitlements and installable offline caching remain separate scope. Suggested first fix: I1, then I3; low-effort catalogue cleanup I6/I7 can follow.

Cloudflare-hosted static tools are feasible; moving the full platform to Workers requires backend/storage/authentication compatibility work and a preservation-tested data migration, not just DNS changes. Current official Workers pricing lists 100,000 free dynamic requests/day and 10 ms CPU per invocation; D1 Free has a 500 MB per-database limit. Free operation of this full workload is not verified. Sources: https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/platform/pricing/ and https://developers.cloudflare.com/d1/platform/limits/. Public-site checks from this environment failed to connect, so current public availability is unconfirmed. ## Resume instructions

1. Read this file, `AGENTS.md`, and `README.md` before changing the app.
2. Inspect current files. Run `npm.cmd test` for the baseline; `npm.cmd run test:e2e` verifies browser workflows when Chrome and development dependencies are available.
3. Preserve `data/chambers.sqlite`, its WAL/SHM files, and `data/records.json`. Do not reset the workspace or create demo accounts in the user's database for testing.
4. Keep the SQL schema in `apps/advocate/database/schema.sql` and `packages/database/schema.sql`. Schema and SQL exports are an explicit user requirement.
5. Continue a specific pending item or new user request. Update this ledger with implementation, test evidence and unresolved issues before ending the session.
6. The runtime server is started with `npm.cmd start` at http://localhost:3000. First-run owner setup is intentionally left for the user; no default credentials exist.

Rules and boundaries: full joining-month fee (waive/add a reduced charge if desired); generation through the current month on opening/refreshing; new batches on existing students and frequency changes begin next month; existing obligations never change when defaults change; skipped paused/inactive periods are not back-billed. Restore rejects older/conflicting histories rather than deleting records. V1 is one academy for the platform owner; optional staff assignment, offline editing, automated delivery, online collection, cloud synchronization and encrypted off-device backups remain unimplemented. WhatsApp only opens drafts and does not report delivery. Excel import uses UTF-8 CSV; PDF output uses browser print. See [app rules and usage](../apps/batchfee-lite/README.md).

**Donation amount in numbers and words (2026-09-27):** Donation Appreciation now offers an optional INR amount with automatically generated, read-only Indian number wording (lakh/crore and exact paise). Positive values from 0.01 through 999999999.99, at most two decimals, are accepted; blank omits the amount. Switching types hides and disables the field, excludes the amount from rendering/validation and retains the value when switching back. All six templates reserve space for numeric amount and words without moving signatures or branding; all exports use the same renderer. **3 certificate browser tests passed**, including exact conversion boundaries, invalid amounts, optional omission, switching, maximum-length/multilingual bounds and PDF/PNG downloads. Donation screenshot reviewed. Live HTTPS HTML/JS bytes match; amount wording, both downloads and 320px layout verified. Static update is live without restart, new dependencies, API writes or database changes. No Git push.

- **External dependency**: needs a provider, account, hosting or deployment choice; not connected.
- **Future scope**: a separate product extension outside the implemented single-chambers app.

## Architecture and recovery notes

- Runtime: Node 22.13+, built-in HTTP/crypto/SQLite. Playwright is development-only. Node 22.17 emits the SQLite experimental-feature warning.
- `server/index.js`: routing and composition; `packages/auth`: shared identity; app `backend/routes.js`: business handlers.
- `apps/advocate/backend/schema.js`: shared form metadata and validation; `apps/advocate/backend/store.js`: persistence, migration and derived state.
- `apps/advocate/database/schema.sql` and `packages/database/schema.sql`: versioned SQL tables, indexes and reporting views. Business records have validated JSON payloads inside SQL; business references are API-enforced. Sessions have a SQL foreign key.
- `apps/advocate/frontend/app.js` / `apps/advocate/frontend/style.css`: UI and themes. No external font/network dependency.
- JSON backups omit user credentials and retain existing users on restore. Full SQL exports include account password hashes and audit history, omit sessions, and must be imported into a new database. See README recovery instructions.
- Concurrent editors must reload stale records. Backups change record versions so stale browser forms cannot overwrite restored records.
- Archived parents retain linked history. New records cannot be linked to archived parents. Cases with linked invoices cannot be reassigned to another client.
- Every active staff member can read every record in the chambers. Clerk billing is read-only; this is not per-case access control.
- Account recovery: a different active owner can reset a team member's password. There is no email-based password recovery or default owner password.
- Snapshot retention is manual. Import limit is 90 MB in the browser. The application loads metadata into memory; very large practices will need paging and storage/backup scaling.
- Git CLI was unavailable in this environment; no commit or repository status claim is made.

# Platform UI continuity review (2026-10-11)

Source review identified different public-home, authenticated-catalogue and Chambers navigation shells, plus app-specific presentation/theme divergence. Recommendation and acceptance criteria are recorded in `docs/UI_PLATFORM_REVIEW_2026-10-11.md`: establish a shared platform shell, distinguish app navigation, simplify the launcher and migrate shared components before extending across apps. At this review checkpoint no UI implementation, rendered browser verification, deployment or business-data changes had been performed. The subsequent implementation and release status at the top of this ledger supersede that checkpoint.
# Free-app card spacing (2026-10-16, deployed)

## Satire Arena reaction and interface refinement (2026-10-17, local)

App-owned UI now renders reaction selections/counts optimistically in tab memory,
decrementing the old choice exactly once and incrementing its replacement. A
Saving state, aria-busy and single-flight writes distinguish pending choices;
confirmed effects/haptics retain the existing preference/reduced-motion contract.
Snapshot rollback plus authoritative refresh handles rejects, cookie requirements,
stale rounds, rate limits and uncertain network failures without replaying writes.
Read revisions prevent older GETs from replacing newer optimistic state. Requests
have a 12-second bound. Transient outage totals are retained as Last synced;
reactions and image sharing stay disabled until successful sync. No localStorage
ballot store, new identity, backend API contract or SQL/schema changes are added.

Existing one-second visible countdown, 30-second polling, midnight IST rollover
and visibility refresh remain and are verified. The signed HttpOnly cookie and
atomic server-side ballot replacement continue to restore saved choices on reload.
Native character-link sharing falls back to clipboard, legacy copy, then a focused
selectable URL; cancellation does not copy. Copy success uses a shared toast timer.
WhatsApp remains an explicit draft action; image sharing excludes personal picks.

App-scoped styles add a distinct selector pill, blurred global header (solid under
reduced transparency), padded summary metrics and dominant-track glow, tactile
actions, selected/pending badges and readable two-row reaction grids. All 78
reaction controls retain labels, focus states and at least 48px touch height.
App/controller/enhancement cache versions are refreshed.

Verification: 15 backend/API checks pass with disposable SQLite fixtures. All 15
distinct Arena Chrome workflows pass across the main and focused runs, including
delayed optimistic writes, changed counts, rollback, stale GET races, cookie/409/
429 failures, actual persisted/exported/removed ballots, native-share cancellation,
clipboard denial/manual fallback, midnight rollover, opt-out/unsupported/reduced
motion haptics, original animations and private-safe PNG exports. One temporary
fixture leak was corrected by deleting synthetic test ballots; the affected SQL
workflow then passed. Two-row layout/overflow checks pass for all 13 characters at
320/390/768/1440px in both themes. Captures reviewed under
`.publish/garland-or-roast-{desktop-dark,mobile-top}.png`. Syntax/diff checks pass.
Implementation is local, not committed or deployed; production votes untouched.

anchors retain native behaviour. The noninteractive status layer does not move

eight-second fallback. Reduced motion shows a stationary bar; print hides it.
No fake percentage, storage, API or app access change. Verification: three shared
shell Chrome workflows pass across focused runs, covering the branded status,
